Hello,

I highly recommend Nathan. He worked tirelessly at Google for years under my supervision. Nathan has great work ethic, intelligence, and a good heart.

One story that perfectly illustrates Nathan's abilities is how he mediated a long-standing dispute between two of our engineers. What started as playful ribbing eventually grew more intense, culminating in a heated debate during one of our daily stand-up meetings. The two engineers, Di and Pierlorenzo, were arguing intensely over resource allocation for their respective parts of our VRDR product.  We call Pierlorenzo "Pier," for short,

Nathan really stepped up. He told me afterwards that he had been silently observing the friction between Di and Pier for months, listening intently to their debates, and realized that the team's success depended on Di and Pier working together. During that tense standup, their argument was as follows: "You need to give me the GCloud resources my work deserves!" Pier shouted, and Di yelled back "No, we need to focus on more compute for the physics simulation!" They got loud enough that one of our downstream teams' manager paused their meeting and looked at us. Other nearby teams also stopped talking and were listening as well. What was a private argument for weeks had become a loud and public scene, and Nathan stepped in to deescalate.

In response to their yelling, Nate smiled and asked, "How are we feeling?" in a very calm tone. While Pier was still shouting, Nathan's calm presence affected Di. Di said softly, "I feel angry. It feels like Pier gets all the attention."

This prompted Pier to quiet down a bit and reply more calmly "I feel angry, too. I also feel neglected and jealous. The team is always prioritizing parts of the code other than my custom tailoring. And Di is the star of the team. She always gets to talk to customers about how cool the physics simulation is, but no one ever gives me credit." Di scoffed at Pier, but kept her voice quieter and calmer. "Yeah right! More like the customers are always complaining at me about how the clothes in the simulation go inside their avatars' bodies. We need to make Blender simulate the interaction way better. You're the one who gets all the glory, Pier."

Nathan had calmed everyone down! Now he smiled and spoke again. "I hear your feelings. It's natural to feel angry, competitive, and jealous. I feel all of those feelings sometimes. But I'm here to tell you that you're both important to the team," he said. "The customers appreciate both of your parts of the project, and so do we. Yanhao has requested and gotten more GCloud resources for both of you. We want you both to succeed."

Pier smiled. When Pier gets angry, he usually stays angry. But Nate had gotten through to him. "Well, that does make me feel better. I'll need some time to cool down completely, but I feel a little better." Di smiled too, and replied "I mean, if Pier can forgive and forget, I will too." Nathan's diplomatic words and calm tone had an immediate effect. He had listened, heard their underlying feelings, probed for some motivations, and reassured them both of their value. While there was still some lingering frustration, the conflict was diffused and the whole floor went back to their normal work.

After the meeting, Pier and Di were chatting amicably. I reassured them that the extra compute resources were on the way, just as Nathan had said. But he had already done most of my job for me. He's a great team player, and your company is lucky to have him

I have not written much about what Nathan is like as an engineer. On the IC side of things, he boosted our test coverage twofold, using LLMs to generate many new test cases, which was very high leverage, and he drew attention to resolving a ton of issues automatically. He also checked those test cases, one by one, himself. Then there was the time he set up data analysis for all customer complaints and grouped them by part of the system and topic, so we all organized our to-dos by how many customers had the same issue. He dove into those customer issues himself in more detail, as well. And he was usually the fastest to finish resolving a ticket, as well. After all, he wrote most of the original VRDR code himself. For years at Majic before Google bought them, it was just him and Vijay building the product. Nathan is one of the best engineers I've ever had the privilege of working with. 

	Again, I recommmend him. He has a strong work ethic, intelligence, technical skills, conflict mediation skills, and a good heart. I chose this story stands out because he took the initiative to keep tabs on his teammates, listen for underlying feelings, and resolve this argument. He did so because he genuinely cares about the team getting along and the project succeeding. This is who Nathan is: a true team player and an asset to any organization.

	Sincerely,
	Yanhao Tan

Step 1: Clean up the beige rectangles

	Use the Background Remover (found under Edit Photo → BG Remover) to isolate the diploma from the uneven beige background.

	Choose a black or white texture overlay.
	Place it over the beige rectangle, then adjust Transparency (top-right corner of the editor) to make it subtle.
	If needed, change the Blend Mode (click on the texture → Edit Photo → Adjust → Blend) to “Multiply” or “Overlay” for a natural look.
This will give you a perfectly uniform beige background with a professional, lightly textured finish.

===================================================================================================
Overview
	To create a Jenkins CI/CD infrastructure for a Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and Large Language Model (LLM) application, you would orchestrate the entire Machine Learning Operations (MLOps) lifecycle using Jenkins as the automation server. The infrastructure would automate data ingestion, model evaluation, and application deployment, rather than Jenkins directly running the LLM itself. 
	A Jenkins pipeline for a RAG/LLM application
	A Jenkins infrastructure for a RAG application can be broken down into a series of automated stages defined in a Jenkinsfile: 
	Continuous Integration (CI): This stage validates changes to the application code, prompts, and model configurations.
	Data Pipeline: Automates the ingestion, preprocessing, and vectorization of documents to update the application's knowledge base.
	Continuous Evaluation: Tests the performance of the LLM and its retrieval system using tools like promptfoo.
	Continuous Deployment (CD): Deploys the RAG application and its updated components to production with strategies like canary or blue/green releases.
	Continuous Monitoring: Observes the live application for issues like data drift and declining performance. 
	Key infrastructure components
	A robust Jenkins infrastructure for a RAG application involves several crucial components: 
	1. Jenkins automation server
	Jenkins Controller and Agents: A central Jenkins controller manages distributed build jobs executed by agents. Some agents may need GPUs for model tasks.
	Credentials Management: The Jenkins server must securely store API keys for LLM providers and other services. 
	2. Version control
	Code and configuration: Use a version control system like Git for application code and Jenkinsfile definitions.
	Model and data: Implement version control for models and evaluation datasets to ensure reproducibility. 
	3. LLM and RAG components
	LLM integration: The pipeline interacts with the LLM via APIs from providers like OpenAI, Anthropic, or by using a local LLM like Llama 2.
	Vector database: A vector store (e.g., a Redis instance with vectors) is needed to retrieve relevant document chunks during the RAG process. Jenkins would manage its updates.
	Data sources: The pipeline needs access to the source data for the RAG knowledge base, such as documents or databases. 
	4. Evaluation and monitoring tools
	Evaluation framework: A tool like promptfoo can be integrated into the Jenkins pipeline to automatically evaluate the LLM's prompts and responses.
	Observability: Implement monitoring to track key LLM performance metrics, such as inference latency and token usage. 
	Example Jenkins pipeline structure
	Here is an example of what a Jenkinsfile might look like for a RAG application, defining the key MLOps steps:
groovy
pipeline {
    agent any

    environment {
        OPENAI_API_KEY = credentials('openai-api-key')
    }

    stages {
        stage('Checkout Code') {
            steps {
                git branch: 'main', url: 'https://github.com/your-org/rag-app.git'
            }
        }

        stage('Data Ingestion & Vectorization') {
            steps {
                script {
                    // Script to pull new documents, parse them,
                    // and update the vector database
                    sh './scripts/ingest_data.sh'
                }
            }
        }

        stage('Build RAG Application') {
            steps {
                script {
                    // Build the RAG application or its Docker image
                    sh 'docker build -t my-rag-app:latest .'
                }
            }
        }

        stage('Evaluate LLM & RAG') {
            steps {
                script {
                    // Use a tool like promptfoo to run automated tests
                    sh 'npm install -g promptfoo'
                    sh 'promptfoo eval -c promptfooconfig.yaml'
                }
            }
        }

        stage('Deploy to Staging') {
            steps {
                script {
                    // Deploy the application to a staging environment
                    sh './scripts/deploy_staging.sh'
                }
            }
        }

        stage('Deploy to Production') {
            when {
                // Only run when evaluation in the previous stage is successful
                expression { currentBuild.result == 'SUCCESS' }
            }
            steps {
                script {
                    // Deploy to production, potentially with a canary or blue/green strategy
                    sh './scripts/deploy_production.sh'
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

===================================================================================================
Tips from my takeaways from the Steve Huynh video: (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c3pzcV9yi24)                                


Focus on the main, most important tasks.

In more depth,

Tips:

1.  2-4 hours of deep work every morning
  a.  If you have multiple to-dos you want to balance in a week, schedule the deep work on different days throughout the week
  b.  Get up early, and consistently.  There's plenty of time in a day to go to sleep early, get up early, and get code done before the workday starts.  Other times are worse for you.   "Early to bed, early to rise, makes a man healthy, wealthy, and wise"
   c.  This will help you not burn out.  

2.  Be willing to say "no" to meetings.     
  a.  Your manager wants you to get SOMETHING done before they expect you to get EVERYTHING done, especially in a more junior role at a good company.  If Steve can keep up with all his responsibilities as an L7, you can do the same.    

3.  Leave your phone in another room, use the KSafe, set up StayFocused on your browser, etc.      
4.  Batch a bunch of chores on the weekends.  But always let yourself sleep early.  "Early to bed, early to rise, makes a man healthy, wealthy, and wise"
5.    Just for me, 			Be calmer.  Usually nothing terrible is going to happen.  Deep breaths, buddy.                
  5b.  Stop changing your mind and getting distracted as much, **_especially_** during the deep work.
  5c.  Just keep the job first  
  5d.  Budget out to see how long your runway is before you NEED another job.  Being (a little) pickier about the roles you apply for and take will help long term success more.  Junior roles at F500 is a good place to start, and FAANGMULASON can be next, but only once you've gotten in the groove at a company with more reasonable expectations.
  5e.  Other priorities (like investment, health, pull-ups, romantic relationship, TV, video games, etc.) have to be put aside if they're interrupting the main priority.  But balance in life is also important.  Steve still was juggling all of those competing priorities he mentioned, which are similar to your own, and he still focused on his main deep work 5 mornings a week.  You can do it!!
	a.   Job family optimization again (finish).    b

Esteemed Herr Professor!

Please forgive a father who is so bold as to turn to you, esteemed Herr Professor, in the interest of his son.

I shall start by telling you that my son Albert is 22 years old, that … he feels profoundly unhappy with his present lack of position, and his idea that he has gone off the tracks with his career & is now out of touch gets more and more entrenched each day. In addition, he is oppressed by the thought that he is a burden on us, people of modest means….

I have taken the liberty of turning [to you] with the humble request to … write him, if possible, a few words of encouragement, so that he might recover his joy in living and working.

If, in addition, you could secure him an Assistant’s position for now or the next autumn, my gratitude would know no bounds….

I am also taking the liberty of mentioning that my son does not know anything about my unusual step.

I remain, highly esteemed Herr Professor,

your devoted

Hermann Einstein

TAILOR THIS LETTER TO each INDIVIDUAL company and reader.     ( I USE THIS name "Jeffrey" because of its similarity to Jeff Kim, a common pseudonym,  and "Jeffrey" starts with the same as the first letter of my dad          )     .                      
    Dear sir or madam,              

    Thank you for your time in reading this personal, humble note.   I have seen my son struggling for months, and despite reaching the final round interview at a few companies, not finding a position.  The job market is very competitive, and it is starting to discourage him.  I noticed the discouragement recently; he is normally an enthusiastic, helpful member of the family, but after interviewing many many times and also seeing some of his friends go through the same struggles, he has become a bit glum.  He graduated from Columbia, learned a lot from his undergraduate classes, and told me he greatly appreciated the education they gave him.  More recently, he worked for JP Morgan Chase and Capital One, doing machine learning and miscellaneous full stack engineering, cloud (AWS), and database where needed for them.  Again, he told me these were great opportunities that he learned a lot from.  I was so proud to see him in these positions.  Which further accentuates how his recent glumness has greatly upset me personally as well.  I want my fine boy to succeed, and his being a shell of his former self because of a few competitive interviews worries me greatly.              
    I understand there is fairness in the interview process, and I'm sure other candidates also deserve this opportunity, but if this letter could at least get some words of encouragement or constructive criticism from you; and ideally, give him a chance to meet you face to face, chat with someone, and/or write some code, that would help his spirits immensely.  He has already submitted hundreds of applications.  Both he and I want him to raise a family of his own, as well, but his recent struggles to find a job have discouraged him.  He would make a fine father, and it is only a temporary setback that has him feeling this way.
    Thank again for your time and effort in this matter...   Probably my sending this note like this is not standard, but I just hate to see my son like this.  I have asked him not to read it, because I don't want to discourage him further.  Again, even a first round chat would help his forward momentum and get him back to his regular, energetic self

	
	Sincerely,
	Jeffrey Bendich

 =======================================================
    COBOL notes:      
 =======================================================
In COBOL, you cannot "dereference" a pointer directly. You have to use the Linkage Section as a "template" or "stencil" and lay it over the memory address.     
This:   is similar to	           CALL "CEEFRST" USING API-ADD-RIGHT, WS-STG-FEEDBACK.                  
		`free(ptr_address)`     in C/C++.

From the tutorial:    https://www.geeksforgeeks.org/cobol/linkage-section-in-cobol/                  
	"Call by Content" means the local variables can't be edited by the linkages in the `LINKAGE SECTION`, but "Call by reference" allows this editing through the LINKAGE SECTION.                       
	The `LINKAGE SECTION.` maps various variable names onto other names.                     
===================================================================================================

"""           The Linkage Section in COBOL is used to declare variables that are passed between programs or subprograms, allowing data to be shared. It does not allocate storage for these variables, as they are expected to be defined in the calling program, and the data is accessed using the CALL statement."""

Tips from my takeaways from the Steve Huynh video: (https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c3pzcV9yi24)                                


Focus on the main, most important tasks.

In more depth,

Tips:

1.  2-4 hours of deep work every morning
  a.  If you have multiple to-dos you want to balance in a week, schedule the deep work on different days throughout the week
  b.  Get up early, and consistently.  There's plenty of time in a day to go to sleep early, get up early, and get code done before the workday starts.  Other times are worse for you.   "Early to bed, early to rise, makes a man healthy, wealthy, and wise"
   c.  This will help you not burn out.  

2.  Be willing to say "no" to meetings.     
  a.  Your manager wants you to get SOMETHING done before they expect you to get EVERYTHING done, especially in a more junior role at a good company.  If Steve can keep up with all his responsibilities as an L7, you can do the same.    

3.  Leave your phone in another room, use the KSafe, set up StayFocused on your browser, etc.      
4.  Batch a bunch of chores on the weekends.  But always let yourself sleep early.  "Early to bed, early to rise, makes a man healthy, wealthy, and wise"
5.    Just for me, 			Be calmer.  Usually nothing terrible is going to happen.  Deep breaths, buddy.                
  5b.  Stop changing your mind and getting distracted as much, **_especially_** during the deep work.
  5c.  Just keep the job first  
  5d.  Budget out to see how long your runway is before you NEED another job.  Being (a little) pickier about the roles you apply for and take will help long term success more.  Junior roles at F500 is a good place to start, and FAANGMULASON can be next, but only once you've gotten in the groove at a company with more reasonable expectations.
  5e.  Other priorities (like investment, health, pull-ups, romantic relationship, TV, video games, etc.) have to be put aside if they're interrupting the main priority.  But balance in life is also important.  Steve still was juggling all of those competing priorities he mentioned, which are similar to your own, and he still focused on his main deep work 5 mornings a week.  You can do it!!
